> SOURCES: 7 files | DKS FY2025 = FYE 2026-01-31; FY2026 = FYE 2027-01-30; Bloomberg labels one year higher; UBS "01/27E" = FY2026; FL standalone "FY2024" = FYE 2025-02-01 | legend: [d]=derived by note-writer, [D]/(derived)=derived by source synthesis author, [r]=table rebuilt from garbled PDF text, =same as=duplicate pointer

## SRC 00_DKS_SYNTHESIS/DKS_Foot_Locker_Merger_Proxy_Projections_Background_and_Fairness.md | proxy dated 2025-07-11 (synthesis undated) | pre-written synthesis of DKS 424B3 (Reg. No. 333-288244; = FL DEFM14A same day) | deal May 2025; FL projections FY2025E-FY2029E; pro formas Q1 FY2025 (13w to 2025-05-03) & FY2024 (to 2025-02-01)
- Drafts checked: S-4 (2025-06-23), S-4/A (2025-07-08); projection tables (mgmt case + additional scenario) identical in all 3; Background identical except S-4/A & 424B3 add Jul 1, 2025 shareholder demand-letter disclosure; nothing removed from final. Local file `01_DKS_SEC_FILINGS/Foot-Locker-merger-docs/DKS_424B3_2025-07-11.md`. "Not in document" = proxy lacks item.

### 1. Deal terms
| Term | Detail |
|---|---|
| Signing | May 15, 2025, announced pre-mkt w/ both cos' preliminary Q1 results |
| Structure | RJS Sub LLC (DKS wholly owned sub) merges into FL; FL survives as wholly owned DKS sub; NYBCL/NYLLCA; Delaware law governs merger agmt |
| Consideration per FL sh (holder election, may differ share-by-share) | (i) $24.00 cash w/o interest or (ii) 0.1168 DKS sh (fixed ratio) |
| Proration/cap | None: "The election is not subject to a minimum or maximum amount of cash consideration or stock consideration." Non-electing → cash |
| Implied stock value | $24.48 on 2025-05-14 (DKS close $209.61); $25.07 on 2025-07-10 (DKS $214.63). FL close $12.87 (5/14/25), $25.32 (7/10/25) |
| Premium | $24.00 = 80.3% to FL close $13.31 (5/13/25); 98.6% to 1-mo VWAP $12.08; 62.6% to 3-mo VWAP $14.76; 32.2% to 6-mo VWAP $18.16; 6.4x TTM EBITDA thru Q1 FY2025 |
| Max DKS sh issuable | up to ~11.8M (incl reserved) or ~11.1M based on FL sh o/s 7/7/25; ex-FL holders ~16.5% of DKS if 100% stock |
| Election mechanics | form mailed ≥20 bus days before deadline to holders of record as of 5th bus day pre-mailing; deadline 5:00pm (exchange agent city) 5 bus days prior to DKS good-faith est closing date; PR announcing deadline ≥3 bus days before; revocable before deadline |
| Fractional sh | cash based on DKS last NYSE sale price last full trading day pre-effective time |
| FL vote | ≥2/3 of outstanding FL sh (NYBCL). Record date 7/7/25: 95,444,721 FL sh, 8,060 holders of record. Special mtg Aug 22, 2025 (virtual). FL D&Os 322,159 sh (~0.34%). DKS owned 4,269,998 FL sh (~4.47%) at record date |
| DKS vote | not required |
| Appraisal | none (NYBCL §910) |
| Tax | taxable to US holders for cash & stock; §304 caveat for holders also owning DKS |
| FL termination fee | $59.5M (2.5% of txn value) — superior proposal, change of rec, willful no-shop breach, or 12-mo tail after no-vote/outside-date/breach termination w/ intervening proposal (50% threshold for tail) |
| DKS reverse termination fee | $95.5M (4%) — outside date w/ only antitrust/injunction conditions unsatisfied, or final non-appealable antitrust injunction |
| Expense reimbursement on FL no-vote | none (DKS asked 1%; FL rejected) |
| No-shop | fiduciary out; "acquisition proposal" >15% equity/<85% retained/>15% assets; "superior proposal" 80%/20%; DKS 5-bus-day match right (2 for later amendments); intervening-event change of rec permitted |
| Regulatory covenant | reasonable best efforts; DKS not required to divest/restrict EXCEPT must accept actions "solely with respect to assets, businesses, or portions of the businesses of Foot Locker that in the aggregate have generated total revenues not in excess of $100,000,000 in the twelve (12)-month period ending February 1, 2025." DKS controls antitrust strategy/timing. HSR filed 2025-06-23. Foreign antitrust clearances req'd (jurisdictions not named) |
| Outside date | May 15, 2026; auto-ext 3 mo (Aug 15, 2026) and again 3 mo (Nov 15, 2026) if only antitrust open |
| Closing conditions | FL vote; no injunction/law; HSR + other antitrust; NYSE listing of DKS sh; S-4 effective; bring-down of reps (MAE std), covenants, no continuing FL MAE |
| MAE | std carve-outs (macro, industry, political, GAAP, law, missing projections per se, credit rating changes, war/pandemic/weather, deal announcement, DKS-requested actions), disproportionate-impact carve-back for (a)-(e),(h) |
| Financing | not conditioned. Cash + 3rd-party debt. Commitment ltr 5/15/25 w/ Goldman Sachs Bank USA up to $2.4B senior unsecured bridge term loans (joinder 5/30/25 added lenders); bridge cut to $2.15B when DKS entered new $2.0B unsecured revolver 6/6/25 (matures 6/6/2030), $250M "certain funds" for merger. Expects to replace bridge w/ senior unsecured notes. Pro formas assume $1.7B unsecured senior notes @6.4% |
| FL Notes | 4.000% Sr Notes due 2029; DKS exchange offer/consent solicitation commenced 6/6/25; requisite consents 6/20/25 (supplemental indenture strips substantially all restrictive covenants, effective at close); exch offer expiry 8/1/25 unless extended. FL credit facilities terminated/paid at close |
| Conduct of business | FL no dividends/buybacks/debt (customary exceptions). DKS no SPECIAL dividends; regular qtrly dividends incl board-approved increases unrestricted |
| D&O | 6-yr tail D&O insurance from DKS |
| Expected close | "second half of 2025" |
| Accounting | acquisition method ASC 805; DKS accounting acquirer |
| Advisors | FL: Evercore (fin), Skadden (legal). DKS: Goldman Sachs (fin), Wachtell Lipton Rosen & Katz "WLRK" (legal). Innisfree proxy solicitor |
| Evercore fee | ~$40M aggregate; $1M on opinion (creditable), rest contingent on close; no Evercore comp from FL/DKS prior 2 yrs |
| Litigation | no lawsuits as of 7/11/25; demand letters from purported FL holders from 7/1/25 alleging S-4 disclosure deficiencies |

### 2. Background timeline
Price progression per FL sh:
| Date | Party | Price/term |
|---|---|---|
| Jun 7, 2024 | DKS ltr | $31–$34 (20–31% prem to $25.88 close 6/6/24), cash or cash/stock mix, subject to diligence, exclusivity requested |
| Sep 5, 2024 | DKS ltr | $37.00, cash or mix |
| Sep 15-16, 2024 | FL board counter | $41.00 (mkt not reflecting Lace-Up; DKS should pay for synergies in all-cash deal) |
| Sep 18, 2024 | DKS | stays $37.00 pending diligence |
| Sep 27, 2024 | DKS (Stack call) | $40.00 "best and final", subject to 60 days diligence from 10/7/24 |
| Sep 29, 2024 | FL board | authorizes diligence "in furtherance of a transaction at $40.00 per share" |
| Nov 15, 2024 | DKS | walks away — couldn't validate mgmt Lace-Up fin case underlying $40 |
| Mar 14, 2025 | DKS ltr | $24.00 (after FL 3/5/25 FY2025 outlook "meaningfully lower" than diligence materials) |
| Apr 1, 2025 | FL board | authorizes $32.00 counter (never conveyed — tariff shock Apr 2-3, FL -15.4% $14.72→$12.45) |
| Apr 4, 2025 | DKS | holds $24.00; discloses ~4.5% FL ownership via open mkt |
| Apr 8-9, 2025 | FL counter | $27.00 uncapped cash/stock election, fixed ratio |
| Apr 16, 2025 | DKS | $25.50 "best and final", stock capped 50% of total consideration |
| Apr 17-18, 2025 | FL board | accepts $25.50; proposes 80% stock cap, election shortly pre-close, better regulatory risk allocation, RTF |
| Apr 24-25, 2025 | agreed | $25.50, 80% cap, election days before close, RTF 4%, FL term fee 2.75%, 1% expense reimbursement on no-vote |
| May 6, 2025 | DKS | cuts to $22.00 after FL weak Q1 prelim; no cap on stock |
| May 10, 2025 | FL counter | $24.00, no cap, term fee 2.5%, no expense reimbursement |
| May 11, 2025 | DKS | accepts $24.00 + all FL terms; announce pre-mkt 5/15 |
| May 14, 2025 | FL board | Evercore fairness opinion; unanimous approval |
| May 15, 2025 | both | signed/announced pre-mkt w/ prelim Q1 results |

Full chronology:
- Pre-Sep 2022 (before Mary Dillon FL CEO 9/1/22): DKS/FL execs "discussed from time to time the potential industrial logic of a combination"; no offers, no MNPI, no price.
- Mar 2023: FL announces "Lace-Up Plan" (tech, loyalty, digital, store refreshes, re-imagined concepts, core banners) w/ "aspirational long-term sales goals and EBIT margin targets."
- Jan 2, 2024: Ed Stack (DKS Exec Chair) messages Dillon re meeting (industry trends, strategic merit).
- Jan 4, 2024 (Chicago area): Dillon + Frank Bracken (FL) meet Ed Stack, Lauren Hobart (DKS CEO), Michael Stack (DKS VP Strategy/Corp Dev; since May 2025 SVP Footwear & Golf Galaxy). Interest: scale, global expansion; DKS "envisioned operating the Foot Locker banner independently from DICK'S Sporting Goods' other banners." Open to all-cash/all-stock/mix.
- Jan 11, 2024: FL board (Evercore, Skadden present): FL "could create more value ... by continuing to focus on the execution of the Lace-Up Plan"; declines. Dillon informs Stack Jan 12.
- Feb 26, 2024: Stack: DKS board concluded further conversation "was warranted." Feb 29: written letter in 2 wks; DKS doing public diligence.
- Mar 6, 2024: FL Q4 2023 results, FY2024 gdnc w/ 2-yr delay in Lace-Up targets; FL $34.31 (3/5) → $24.24 (3/6).
- Mar 12, 2024: DKS letter assumes FL not interested given mkt reaction; price "would need to reflect the risk and work required to execute the Lace-Up Plan."
- Apr 11, 2024 (Paris vendor mtgs): Stack tells Dillon DKS "was exploring other potential strategic options"; requests mtg w/ Dona Young (FL Chair).
- Apr 23, 2024: JD Sports agrees to acquire Hibbett; Stack asks to speak. Apr 25: agree to meet May 1.
- May 1, 2024 (Chicago): Young, Dillon meet Stack, Hobart. DKS open to all-cash or election cash vs cash/stock mix. FL: stock "did not appropriately reflect the value of successfully executing the Lace-Up Plan"; not right time.
- May 10, 2024: DKS board met; engaging fin advisor, legal, consultant; written proposal after DKS 5/29 and FL 5/30 earnings calls.
- May 20, 2024 FL board: reviews "the complexities and risks to achieving the Lace-Up Plan, the differences between Wall Street analyst estimates and the targets in the Lace-Up Plan, and recent investor feedback ... regarding the timing and achievability"; structural risk: "relies heavily on a single product category, with merchandise sourced from a limited number of brand partners"; sensitivities.
- Jun 7, 2024: DKS letter $31–$34 (20–31% prem to $25.88), cash or mix; price may adjust post-diligence; wants to speak w/ "a brand partner that DICK'S Sporting Goods considered to be important to the success of Foot Locker"; exclusivity; response by 6/21. Same day FL signs Evercore engagement.
- Jun 10, 2024 FL board: mgmt's latest forecasts "and two illustrative sensitivity cases"; two-phase diligence (limited first; more only if DKS raises price); authorizes NDA.
- Jun 14–Jul 25, 2024 NDA: FL proposed 24-mo standstill; DKS exclusivity + 3-mo (6/17); Skadden 18 mo no exclusivity (6/18); DKS refused >3 mo (6/19), "very serious"; FL floated 12 mo (6/20); 6/21 mtg cancelled. Jul 12: DKS drops exclusivity, holds standstill, "prepared to make its proposal to acquire Foot Locker public" if FL won't engage. Jul 15: Stack 4 mo max. Jul 18: FL agrees 4 mo. Jul 25, 2024: NDA signed, 4-mo standstill, no "don't ask, don't waive."
- Jul 26, 2024 (Palm Beach): mgmt mtg w/ Goldman, Evercore; FL presents Lace-Up, aggregated fin model thru FY2028, tech, real estate, new formats.
- Aug 28, 2024: FL Q2 2024; stock $32.81→$29.45.
- Sep 5, 2024: DKS $37.00, cash or mix; response by 9/16.
- Sep 15, 2024 FL board: Skadden fiduciary duties; Evercore prelim valuation + pro forma cap structure; alternatives: Lace-Up execution, JVs, divestitures, acquisitions, operational transformation, cap structure/return changes, capital mkts txns, minority investments (incl private investors), licensing, other strategic/financial buyers. Prefers all-cash; DKS should pay more as DKS "would retain the benefits of potential synergies in an all-cash transaction"; FL "was undervalued by the market ... because the market was not taking into account the Lace-Up Plan." Instructs $41.00 counter.
- Sep 16, 2024: Dillon conveys $41; Stack: DKS "may not be willing to pay $41.00." Sep 18: DKS stays $37 pending Lace-Up diligence.
- Sep 23, 2024 FL board: Dillon on "the recently announced CEO succession at an important Foot Locker brand partner" and impact on FL results. No further diligence "until DICK'S Sporting Goods put its best foot forward on price"; Evercore tells Goldman (9/25).
- Sep 27, 2024: Stack $40.00 "best and final," 60 days diligence from 10/7; response by 9/30.
- Sep 29, 2024 FL board: CEO succession and "performance issues at an important Foot Locker brand partner"; CFO Baughn: "weaker than expected operating performance in September, particularly in Foot Locker's international business." Evercore compares $37 vs $40. Decides not to contact other bidders "in light of the risk of a public disclosure of such outreach and the low likelihood that other strategic acquirers would have strategic interest in, or the financial capacity required to complete, an acquisition of Foot Locker." Authorizes diligence at $40.
- Oct–early Nov 2024: "Extensive due diligence" (DKS, advisors, consultants), two all-day NY mgmt mtgs, online data room, "including a focus on its execution of the Lace-Up Plan."
- Oct 31, 2024: WLRK initial draft merger agmt. Nov 1 FL board: Q3 likely low end of gdnc; "continued weakness at an important Foot Locker brand partner." Skadden flags: no antitrust commitment, no RTF, expense reimbursement on no-vote. Nov 6: Skadden draft (lower term fee, enhanced antitrust efforts, RTF, no expense reimbursement).
- Nov 15, 2024: DKS terminates. Stack: diligence "revealed that Foot Locker required a much more significant turnaround effort than it had anticipated"; DKS "had not been able to validate Foot Locker management's financial case that is core to successful execution of the Lace-Up Plan and was the basis for the $40.00 per share proposal." Nov 18/26: FL confidential info destroyed.
- Dec 4, 2024: FL Q3 2024; FY2024 sales & EPS gdnc lowered; stock $24.17→$22.02.
- Feb 18, 2025 FL board: Lace-Up progress; inflation, consumer sentiment, "lack of innovation in athletic apparel and footwear categories and lagging international consumer confidence."
- Mar 5, 2025: FL Q4 2024 + 2025 outlook: "ongoing pressure on margin recovery and another year of significant investment." "The outlook for 2025 was meaningfully lower than financial information shared with DICK'S Sporting Goods during its due diligence review in October and November of 2024."
- Mar 13-14, 2025: DKS letter $24.00: FL falling stock & analyst reports "reflected a lack of confidence in Foot Locker's ability to achieve the Lace-Up Plan on a stand-alone basis," consistent w/ Nov conclusions; DKS "had the expertise, brand partner relationships and credibility to restore Foot Locker to its rightful position"; $24 "reflected the significant work, effort and investment that would be required"; limited confirmatory diligence; response by 3/20; "time was of the essence."
- Mar 16, 2025: Stack: won't transact at pre-Nov price; "significant investment would be required by DICK'S Sporting Goods to meaningfully improve the state of the Foot Locker business."
- Mar 19, 2025 FL board: requests analysis of LT plan assumptions (consumer sentiment, "delays in the repositioning of an important Foot Locker brand partner," macro, structural risks), alternatives (divestitures, partnerships, op transformation, cap structure, cap mkts, minority investments, licensing), other acquirers. Mar 21: Dillon asks for time; Stack: absent engagement DKS "would consider making public its proposal."
- Mar 23, 2025 FL board: reviews margin, brand-partner sales, banner/geography growth assumptions; headwinds "appeared to be worse than Foot Locker management had expected" at 2/18. Instructs LT forecast of "the most likely base case in the current environment through fiscal year 2028" w/ lower margin expansion, lower brand-partner sales, slower intl growth.
- Mar 25, 2025 FL board: mgmt presents "management case" (EBIT margin, brand-partner sales, "a re-allocation of capital investment from Foot Locker's international businesses to Foot Locker's more profitable domestic banners"). "Wall Street analyst estimates for Foot Locker were lower than the management case." Deemed "a reasonable estimate"; Evercore to use.
- Apr 1, 2025 FL board: Evercore prelim valuation of $24 (trading comps, precedents, DCF, all-cash). Weighs delaying for tariff visibility vs DKS going public; value of cash/stock election. Authorizes $32 counter.
- Apr 2-3, 2025: tariffs; FL -15.4% ($14.72→$12.45); Dillon, Young, advisors delay conveying $32.
- Apr 4, 2025: Dillon: "not the right time." Stack: DKS "did not intend to lower its offer price of $24.00," open cash/stock/mix; DKS "had acquired Foot Locker shares through open market purchases, and ... currently owned approximately 4.5% of Foot Locker's common stock." Feedback by 4/8.
- Apr 7, 2025: Evercore/Goldman call on election; Goldman: "minimal diligence."
- Apr 8, 2025 FL board: Evercore reviews combo metrics, implied exchange ratios, "potential sources of synergies," stock-election value "under varying assumptions with respect to synergies and trading multiples." Uncapped election valued (participate in retail recovery & synergies; DKS has "a broader portfolio of product offerings, less brand partner concentration and a wider customer base"). Counter $27.00 uncapped, fixed ratio (conveyed 4/9).
- Apr 16, 2025: Stack $25.50 "best and final," stock capped 50%; maybe flexibility on structure, later little confidence DKS board would modify.
- Apr 17-18, 2025 FL board: accepts $25.50; proposes 80% cap, election shortly pre-close, better regulatory risk allocation, fee to FL if no regulatory approval.
- Apr 19-24, 2025: advisor calls on election mechanics, regulatory efforts, fees. Apr 21: Goldman reaffirms $25.50, 80% cap, election at shareholder mtg, RTF, expense reimbursement on no-vote. Apr 24: DKS revised — election "in the days immediately prior to closing."
- Apr 25, 2025: Dillon note: agreed $25.50, fixed ratio, 80% cap, election pre-close, RTF 4%, term fee 2.75%, 1% expense reimbursement on no-vote. Apr 26: WLRK draft. Apr 29: FL data room opened. Apr 30: Skadden draft; FL posts prelim Q1 2025 info.
- May 1, 2025: Stack: DKS mgmt "very concerned by Foot Locker's lower than anticipated financial performance in the first quarter which indicated that Foot Locker's business was declining more rapidly than DICK'S Sporting Goods had expected."
- May 2, 2025 (NY diligence): DKS asks FL to accelerate prelim Q1 to coincide w/ announcement.
- May 6, 2025: Stack lowers to $22.00 citing Q1 and "the achievability of second half 2025 targets"; no cap on stock election.
- May 7, 2025: Bracken meets CEO of "Company X" at industry event (ongoing talks re separate business txn); Company X "would not be interested in acquiring Foot Locker" but "would potentially be interested in a minority investment with board designation rights and exploring a global partnership"; never followed up w/ Dillon. Same day WLRK draft at $22/no cap; NDA made mutual for FL reverse diligence.
- May 8, 2025 FL board: Q1 + prelim FY2025: "negative consumer trends, the continued underperformance of an important Foot Locker brand partner, weak apparel sales and international results that were significantly below the management case." Considers "continued pressure through fiscal year 2026 on an important Foot Locker brand partner," tariffs, "the increasing likelihood that Foot Locker would significantly underperform the management case for the year." Company X "vague and uncertain," worth less than DKS deal.
- May 10, 2025 FL board: Evercore prelim valuation of $22 (mgmt case); reviews "additional scenario" and "for information purposes only" Evercore DCF on it. Company X "highly dilutive." Counter $24.00, no cap, term fee 2.5% (from 2.75%), no expense reimbursement; "move quickly."
- May 11, 2025: Stack accepts all; announce pre-open 5/15.
- May 12, 2025: FL reverse diligence call on DKS incl DKS anticipated Q1. May 11-14 final agmt negotiated.
- May 14, 2025 FL board: Human Capital Cttee approves retention plan for critical employees; Audit Cttee approves prelim Q1 PR. Evercore: 80.3% prem to 5/13 close, 98.6% to 1-mo VWAP, 6.4x TTM EBITDA; oral + written fairness opinion. Evercore analysis "did not consider the potential value to shareholders from electing to receive DICK'S Sporting Goods common stock ... or the potential value of the election itself." Skadden reviews regulatory covenant "which included commitments by DICK'S Sporting Goods to divest assets of Foot Locker generating revenues of up to $100 million." Unanimous; 2.5% fee "sufficiently low so as to not preclude any offers to acquire Foot Locker at a higher price."
- May 15, 2025: announced pre-mkt; both release prelim Q1.
- Other bidders: none contacted; only "Company X" (unnamed) — declined acquisition, floated minority investment/global partnership rejected as dilutive/vague. Board twice (9/29/24 and Reasons section) declined market check (leak risk, low strategic interest/capacity). DKS repeatedly said prepared to go public.

### 3. FL management projections
- Only 2 five-yr FL standalone tables, 3 lines each (Rev, Adj EBITDA, UFCF). Not in document: comps, GM, EBIT, NI, EPS, capex, D&A, WC, tax rate, levered FCF; no DKS standalone, no combined, no quantified synergy estimate. EPS inputs for Evercore PV-of-future-price referenced but not disclosed.
- 3a Management case ("Forecasts"): developed per board 3/23/25 instruction, presented 3/25/25 as "the most likely base case in the current environment through fiscal year 2028"; used by board, approved for Evercore, provided in part to DKS: "a subset of the Foot Locker management financial projections to DICK'S Sporting Goods (excluding 2029 estimates and unlevered free cash flow estimates)." Street lower than this case (3/25/25).

| $M | FY25E | FY26E | FY27E | FY28E | FY29E |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue | 8,000 | 8,246 | 8,523 | 8,828 | 8,986 |
| Adj EBITDA | 453 | 591 | 702 | 776 | 830 |
| UFCF | 135 | 151 | 285 | 288 | 339 |
- Synthesis-derived: rev growth 3.1%/3.4%/3.6%/1.8%; Adj EBITDA mgn 5.7%/7.2%/8.2%/8.8%/9.2%; UFCF/EBITDA 30%/26%/41%/37%/41%. Ref: FL FY2024 (to 2/1/25) net sales $7,988M, income from ops $103M.
- Assumptions: EBIT margin, brand-partner sales "including sales of products from an important Foot Locker brand partner," "a re-allocation of capital investment from Foot Locker's international businesses to Foot Locker's more profitable domestic banners."
- 3b Additional Scenario (downside): after "the announcement of tariffs and associated market turmoil and potential consumer impacts, a slower recovery by an important Foot Locker brand partner, and weaker than anticipated first quarter results"; "revenue growth of approximately 1% per year for fiscal years 2026-2028 and limited the degree of EBIT margin expansion." Considered by board; to Evercore "for its reference," used only in informational "Other Factors."

| $M | FY25E | FY26E | FY27E | FY28E | FY29E |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue | 7,951 | 8,023 | 8,103 | 8,184 | 8,225 |
| Adj EBITDA | 432 | 534 | 619 | 685 | 731 |
| UFCF | 59 | 138 | 228 | 272 | 309 |
- Synthesis-derived: rev growth 0.9%/1.0%/1.0%/0.5%; EBITDA mgn 5.4%/6.7%/7.6%/8.4%/8.9%; vs mgmt case FY28E rev -$644M (-7.3%), Adj EBITDA -$91M (-11.7%).
- 3c Street case: not a table; Evercore applied multiples to consensus FY2025E Adj EPS & Adj EBITDA (values undisclosed) → $10.85–$17.00 (P/E), $10.25–$20.45 (EV/EBITDA).
- 3d Not disclosed: Jun 10, 2024 "management's most recent forecasts and two illustrative sensitivity cases"; FL aggregated model thru FY2028 shared 7/26/24 and Lace-Up fin case behind $40 that DKS couldn't validate. Mar 5, 2025 outlook "meaningfully lower than financial information shared with DICK'S Sporting Goods during its due diligence review in October and November of 2024."
- 3e Caveats: "These Foot Locker management financial projections are not, and should not be viewed as, public guidance or targets."; "Modeling and forecasting the future performance of a specialty athletic footwear and apparel retail company is a highly speculative endeavor."; projections "do not take into account any circumstances or events occurring after the date they were prepared, including the announcement of the merger and merger-related expenses."; not per SEC/AICPA, not audited/reviewed by KPMG (FL) or Deloitte (DKS); non-GAAP not reconciled ("Financial measures provided to a financial advisor are excluded from the SEC's definition of 'non-GAAP financial measures.'"); no intent to update; Evercore "expressed no view as to the Forecasts or the assumptions on which they were based."

### 4. Evercore fairness opinion (for FL; Annex B)
- Dated 5/14/25; merger consideration fair to FL holders (ex cancelled/converted sh). Opinion "did not address the stock election (or the decision of any holder of Foot Locker common stock related thereto) or the stock consideration"; all analyses vs $24.00 cash. Mkt data as of 5/13/25. Evercore "was not authorized to solicit, and did not solicit, interest from any third party."
- Goldman opinion for DKS: not in document (no DKS-side opinion/analyses/projections; DKS vote not req'd).
- Inputs: FL net debt $106M at 5/3/25 (total debt + debt-like ex pension, less cash), $49M at 2/1/25 (for DCF/LBO); unconsolidated assets $115M; FD shares via TSM (sh, options, PSUs, RSUs, DSUs at 5/12/25).
- 4a Trading comps. Peers: Athletic Specialty = Academy, DKS, JD Sports; Footwear = Caleres, Designer Brands, Genesco, Shoe Carnival; Apparel Specialty = A&F, AEO, Gap, Urban Outfitters.

Historical avg multiples (5/13/25, consensus):
| Benchmark | Current | L6M | L1Y | L2Y | L3Y | L5Y |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Athletic P/NTM EPS | 9.7x | 9.8x | 10.7x | 10.5x | 9.9x | 11.3x |
| Athletic TEV/NTM EBITDA | 5.2x | 5.4x | 5.8x | 5.5x | 5.3x | 5.5x |
| Footwear P/NTM EPS | 11.2x | 10.4x | 11.8x | 10.5x | 9.0x | 10.4x |
| Footwear TEV/NTM EBITDA | 3.8x | 4.4x | 4.9x | 4.7x | 4.4x | 5.0x |
| Apparel P/NTM EPS | 10.3x | 10.1x | 11.3x | 12.4x | 12.8x | 14.4x |
| Apparel TEV/NTM EBITDA | 5.1x | 5.2x | 5.8x | 5.8x | 5.4x | 5.6x |
| FL P/NTM EPS | 9.3x | 10.7x | 12.3x | 12.4x | 11.2x | 10.6x |
| FL TEV/NTM EBITDA | 2.9x | 3.9x | 4.5x | 4.3x | 4.1x | 3.9x |

FY2025E multiples (consensus) mean/median: Athletic P/Adj EPS 10.1x/7.8x, TEV/Adj EBITDA 5.8x/5.0x; Footwear 12.3x/13.0x, 3.9x/4.1x; Apparel 10.6x/10.5x, 4.9x/4.6x; Total 11.1x/10.4x, 4.8x/4.1x. FL: 9.8x P/Adj EPS, 3.0x TEV/Adj EBITDA.

### 5. Reasons / negative factors
- DKS board reasons: not in document (no DKS vote). DKS rationale only indirectly: Jan 4, 2024 (scale, global expansion, operate FL banner independently); Mar 14, 2025 letter ("expertise, brand partner relationships and credibility to restore Foot Locker to its rightful position in the industry"); risk factors ("DICK'S Sporting Goods expects to operate Foot Locker as a standalone business unit within its portfolio and maintain the Foot Locker brands and expects to grow the business by applying operational expertise"; "DICK'S Sporting Goods expects significant cost synergies").
- FL board positives (5/14/25, unanimous): (1) consideration vs pre-approval prices; (2) premium 80.3%/98.6%/62.6%/32.2%, 6.4x TTM EV/EBITDA; (3) election w/o proration; (4) cash downside protection; (5) fixed ratio lets stock electors gain from DKS appreciation pre-close; (6) upside incl "the potential to realize synergies"; (7) de-risking strategic plan — board "remains supportive of" Lace-Up but cites macro/tariff/recession risk, "softer consumer traffic trends globally"; "Foot Locker purchased 85% of its merchandise in 2024 from its five top suppliers, with approximately 59% purchased from its largest supplier. Each of its banners is highly dependent on Foot Locker's largest supplier"; ongoing store-refresh capex; omnichannel tech investment; promo pressure on merch margins; non-US "have underperformed Foot Locker's U.S. businesses and will require significant investments, particularly in Europe"; (8) de-risk model ("relies heavily on a single product category, with merchandise sourced from a limited number of brand partners and sales highly dependent on consumer enthusiasm for new and innovative product offerings"); DKS "has a broader portfolio of product offerings, less brand partner concentration and a wider customer base"; (9) standalone stock uncertainty incl missing gdnc/Lace-Up; (10) "no other alternatives were reasonably likely to create greater value"; (11) negotiation outcomes (fee size, regulatory risk allocation, RTF, no fee on no-vote, election just pre-close no cap/proration) — "was the best transaction that could be obtained ... at the time"; (12) expected cost synergies (no amount; "no assurance that any particular amount"); (13) Evercore opinion (not covering election); (14) likelihood of close (no financing condition; DKS regulatory efforts); (15) terms: fiduciary out, $59.5M fee not likely to deter, $95.5M RTF, no fee on no-vote, specific performance; (16) timing: soliciting others "would jeopardize or, at a minimum, delay the merger"; can consider unsolicited until vote; (17) DKS business/reputation (reverse diligence); (18) DKS committed financing.
- Negatives: cash electors forgo Lace-Up/combined upside; stock electors value variance (could be <$24); non-completion risk; $59.5M fee may deter; disruption/distraction/attrition/costs; interim restrictions; uncertainty for "employees, vendors, suppliers, landlords" + litigation; HSR & non-US antitrust "may be delayed or not granted"; risk factors; insider interests. Conclusion: negatives "were significantly outweighed"; merger "would maximize the immediate value of Foot Locker shareholders' shares and minimize the risks and uncertainty affecting the future prospects of Foot Locker, including the potential execution risks associated with the Lace-Up Plan."

### 7. Insider interests (assume close + qualifying termination 6/30/25; $24.00; PSUs at target)
- ITM options cashed ($24 − strike); OTM cancelled (no insider held unvested ITM options). Employee RSUs/PSUs (PSUs at level per agreements) → DKS time-based RSUs at 0.1168x; exec converted RSUs vest fully on qualifying termination within 2 yrs (double trigger; prorated for awards granted after 5/15/25). Non-employee director RSUs/DSUs cashed at $24. Severance 2.0x (base + target bonus) all execs (Dillon per employment agmt; others Exec Severance Policy) + prorated bonus (if ≥6 mo elapsed), 3 mo COBRA differential, outplacement for Dillon. Execs ex-Dillon: non-compete/non-solicit 1 yr post-termination regardless of CIC. No post-closing DKS employment/comp agreements as of 7/11/25. Retention plan approved 5/14/25; PF assumes $7.5M paid 6 mo post-close.

### 8. Merger risk factors
- Nike: word "Nike" absent from proxy; only "an important Foot Locker brand partner"/"its largest supplier"; quantification only 85% top-5 / ~59% largest supplier (2024). No merger-specific risk factor re brand-partner consent/CIC/allocation; generic: "Completion of the merger may trigger change in control provisions in certain agreements to which Foot Locker is a party"; counterparties "may require a fee for such waivers or seek to renegotiate the agreements on terms less favorable."
- Merger risks: (1) fixed ratio — stock electors "cannot be sure of the value of the consideration they will receive"; cash fixed $24; (2) conditions (2/3 vote, HSR, foreign antitrust) may fail; outside date 5/15/26 ext to 8/15/26, 11/15/26; $95.5M RTF; (3) regulators "may impose conditions that could have an adverse effect"; (4) insider interests; (5) no-shop + $59.5M fee "may discourage other companies from trying to acquire Foot Locker"; (6) interim restrictions; (7) relationships: "customers, suppliers and other persons ... may delay or defer certain business decisions or might decide to terminate, change or renegotiate their relationships," incl "an adverse effect on DICK'S Sporting Goods' ability to realize the expected synergies"; (8) key personnel; (9) "significant non-recurring costs"; "this net benefit may not be achieved in the near term, or at all"; (10) failure hurts FL; (11) termination fees; (12) PF preliminary, PPA may differ materially; (13) CIC provisions; (14) litigation (demand letters from 7/1/25).
- DKS post-close risks: (15) dilution up to ~16.5% "may result in fluctuations in the market price of DICK'S Sporting Goods common stock, including a DICK'S Sporting Goods common stock price decrease"; ex-FL holder selling; (16) financing: DKS "cannot guarantee that it will be able to obtain such financing on favorable terms"; (17) synergies/integration: "DICK'S Sporting Goods may be unable to successfully operate the Foot Locker business or realize the cost synergies or other anticipated benefits of the merger." "Although DICK'S Sporting Goods expects significant cost synergies and other benefits to result from the merger, there can be no assurance that DICK'S Sporting Goods will actually realize any of them, or realize them within the anticipated timeframe. DICK'S Sporting Goods expects to operate Foot Locker as a standalone business unit within its portfolio and maintain the Foot Locker brands and expects to grow the business by applying operational expertise." Challenges: "operating multiple banners, a differentiated store concept and successfully addressing the challenges facing Foot Locker's business"; "coordinating operations in countries in which DICK'S Sporting Goods has not previously operated"; (18) "no assurances that the combined company ... will realize the expected operating efficiencies, cost savings or other benefits"; (19) Stack family Class B controls majority of combined voting power, "would control the outcome of a vote on any corporate transaction"; (20) different holder rights (Delaware docs); (21) different stock drivers; (22) leverage ~$7.38B → up to ~$13.03B (=above); consequences: rate exposure on "variable rate term facilities," competitive disadvantage, constrained borrowing, cash to debt service; "While DICK'S Sporting Goods is focused on maintaining investment grade ratings from these agencies, it may be unable to do so."

### 9. Proxy vs earnings calls (synthesis's list; items =same as §§1-8 unless noted)
- FL 5-yr plan & downside (=§3; DKS hasn't disclosed FL projections in own filings); DKS got only FY25E-FY28E rev & Adj EBITDA; Street below mgmt case + 5/8/25 board "increasing likelihood ... significantly underperform"; DKS price path (=§2; held $24 through April tariff shock); Nov 2024/Mar 2025 diligence conclusions; ~4.5% open-mkt stake (=§1/§6; PF gain $46.9M/$32.5M); DKS original intent (independent banner, global expansion, brand-partner contact request); DKS threatened to go public ≥3 times (Jul 12, 2024; Mar 21, 2025; implied Apr 1, 2025), refused standstill >4 mo; $100M divestiture cap, HSR 6/23/25, RTF $95.5M; financing (~$110M annual interest on $1.7B @6.4%); leverage $7.38B→$13.03B; PF FY24 dil EPS $12.61/$11.91 vs $14.05, Q1 FY25 PF $(1.52)/$(1.12); purchase acctg goodwill only $289M/$118M on ~$2.3B/~$2.0B, $105M PP&E step-up, $72M fav lease, FL trademarks $210M (cash); one-time costs ($56.4M, $55.3M, $22.6M, $7.5M, $2.0M); supplier concentration 85%/~59%; FL board's internal view ("the structural risks inherent in Foot Locker's business model," intl "significantly below the management case," pressure thru FY2026 on brand partner, "lack of innovation"); only Company X, no market check; Evercore ranges (all comps, LBO, premiums below $24; mgmt DCF & precedents bracket; PTs top at $24; fee ~$40M mostly contingent).
- Election timing: closes 5 bus days pre-est close → FL holders choose w/ near-final DKS price knowledge; no cap/proration → actual mix (DKS debt & share issuance) set only at close; stock worth $25.07 vs $24 cash on 7/10/25.
- DKS dividend covenant: regular dividends + increases unrestricted; specials prohibited. Expected close 2H25; outside date 5/15/26 + two 3-mo antitrust ext.
- Not in document: quantified cost synergies, DKS/combined projections, Goldman DKS-side opinion, DKS board reasons, comparative per-share data, Nike by name, FL comps/GM/EPS/capex projections, foreign antitrust jurisdictions.

- Price $137.34 (2026-10-05 close per README; Yahoo intraday $137.26); mkt cap ≈$12.4B (90.1M dil sh, Q2 FY26 10-Q); EV ex-leases ≈$13.4B. DKS fiscal labels (FY2026 = FYE 2027-01-30 = BBG "2027 Y"; BBG labels one higher: BBG "2026 Y"=DKS FY2025, BBG "2027 Q3"=DKS Q3 FY26; UBS "01/27E"=FY2026). FL standalone FY ends near Jan 31 (FL "FY2024" = FYE 2025-02-01). Nike FY ends May 31 (Nike "1Q FY27" = Jun–Aug 2026 ≈ DKS Q2/early Q3 FY26). "(derived)" = primer author's arithmetic; source files cited (`_primer_notes/S01…S20b`, `reconciliation.md` w/ 65 discrepancies D1–D65 + canonical values, `timeline.md`, `00_orientation.md`, `01_orchestrator_working_analysis.md`, `dks_scenario_model.py`).
- FL closed 2025-09-08 (mid Q3 FY25). Two segments since: DICK'S Business (DSG, House of Sport, Field House, Golf Galaxy, Going Going Gone!, Public Lands, GameChanger) and Foot Locker Business (Foot Locker, Kids Foot Locker, Champs Sports, WSS, atmos). FL comps "pro forma" until FL enters comp base Q4 FY2026.
- Comp definition changed 3x: FY2022 (relocated stores incl), FY2024 (GameChanger rev incl; renamed "comparable sales"), FY2025 (Warehouse Sale stores incl; DICK'S Media Network named).

### Executive summary
- What DKS is: largest US sporting-goods retailer; FY2025 DICK'S Business sales $14.11B, 11.12% segment margin; comps +4.5% (FY25), +6.0% (Q1 FY26), +4.9% (Q2 FY26). Bought FL Sept-2025 for $2.51B; FL ~2,212-store global sneaker chain, ~$7.4–7.5B FY26 sales guide, losing money: segment profit −$31.9M Q2 FY26, FY26 guide −$80M to −$40M. Stack-family Class B 23.57M sh × 10 votes ≈78% of votes (derived).
- What happened: 2026-08-25 FY26 non-GAAP EPS gdnc cut $13.50–14.50 → $11.00–12.00; OI −$260M at midpoint (FL −$190M, core −$70M); tax rate ~29%. Stock −30.7% on 38.85M sh, after −25% prior 2 months; ASO −5.8% same day. Since: 7 insiders bought $5.59M (VWAP $130.59) incl CFO $999,983, not CEO or Chairman; DKS issued $1B 6.2%/6.9% 2036/2056 notes ("2026 debt"; no 2026 maturity exists), uses incl "repurchases of shares"; short interest 13.7% of float.
- Street: "Core fine, FL broken, footwear cycle clears in 2027." 11 Buy, 5 Hold, 1 Underperform (BMO, $110); mean PT ~$158. Cons FY26 EPS $11.49, FY27 $13.36 (+16%) on rev +2.3% (margin-recovery call). 11.9x FY26E / 10.3x FY27E; 3.6% div yield; traded 17–18x fwd at Jan-2025 and Jan-2026 peaks.
- Author call: LONG, half position now.
| Scenario | Prob | FY27 EPS | Multiple | Value/move |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Base | 50% | $12.67 | 12x | ~$154, +12% (+3.6% yield) |
| Bull | 25% | $14.93 | 14x | ~$209, +52% |
| Bear | 25% | $10.42 | 9x | ~$93, −32% |
- Prob-weighted ~$152 (+11%; ~+14–15% total return); ~1.6:1 skew (derived).
- Variant 1 "The core is not the safe half" (bearish vs Street): in 4 post-close qtrs DICK'S segment margin fell y/y in 3 despite 3–6% comps; Q2 FY26 ≈−96bp ex tariff refunds; SG&A levered 1 of 7 qtrs; capex ~2.3x D&A; ~half of comp from relocations (Wells: underlying +1.0–1.5%). Author FY27 core OI ~$1.60B vs ~$1.65B cons; FY27 EPS ~$12.4–12.7 pre-buyback vs $13.36.
- Variant 2 "FL is a call option priced as a liability" (bullish): SOTP implies FL ≈ −$1.1B (derived). Losses concentrated in severable pieces: International (681 stores, −8.1% comps, ~4pts group tax-rate drag ≈ $0.65/sh); ~$0.4B/yr FL capex; Champs overlap. Mgmt signalling: Stack "simply yes" to rightsizing/monetizing and "menu" of options; new Aldi/Tesco operator running International who bought $0.5M stock; $1B liquidity raised at low. FL to ~zero value ≈ +$12/sh. Buyback ≈ +$0.75 FY27 EPS per $1B = second lever.
- Short term: Nov-24 print (company "tentatively"; vendors list Nov-25): Q3 EPS base ~$1.20 vs $1.39 cons, P(beat) 40%; DICK'S comp P(≥1.69% cons) 65%, post-lap card data imply ~0–5% reported growth; FY guide P(held/narrowed) 75%, P(cut below $11) 20%; P(concrete FL action) 40%; P(stock up on day) ~50%, options imply ±9.5–10%. March-2027 FY27 guide: 65% prob midpoint below cons — main near-term risk. P(≥$500M buyback/ASR by March 2027) 55%.
- Change mind: vs V1 — core SG&A levers at ≤2% comp; vs V2 — mgmt reaffirms full global FL footprint or keeps raising FL capex; vs long — Nike Investor Day (Nov 16–17) dilutes FL NA allocation value, or FL Q4 comps stay negative vs clearance compare.
- Top diligence gaps: FL P&L by geography/banner (not disclosed); HoS/relocation cohort comps; real-time Q3 promo tracker; IR on use of $1B; Nike 2027 allocation plans.

### 1. How DKS makes money
- Largest US sporting-goods retailer (~$14.1B FY25 DICK'S Business sales; ~9% share of ~$140B US "core" TAM per Mar-2026 investor deck). Nike ~25% of core purchases; total co 31% FY2025 incl ~5 mo FL (FY25 10-K). ~13% owned vertical brands (DSG, CALIA, VRST, Maxfli…). ~890 large-format stores fulfil most online orders. Profit from (i) merch margin on full-price allocated/"high-heat" nat'l brand + vertical brands, (ii) leverage of largely fixed occupancy/labor, (iii) small high-margin alt profit pools (GameChanger SaaS, DICK'S Media Network). FL segment: mall/urban sneaker specialist, ~2,212 owned stores in 20+ countries, low-20s/high-20s GM, currently loses money.
- Store counts (10-K FY25; Q2 FY26 10-Q):
| Banner | FYE 2/1/25 | FYE 1/31/26 stores / sq ft M | 8/1/26 | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DSG legacy box | 677 (restated) | 644 / 34.4 | 630 | ~50K sq ft "store of stores" |
| Field House | 27 (restated from 26) | 42 / 2.4 | 52 | ~50K sq ft w/ HoS elements; "the workhorse" (Gupta, Goldman 2025-09-04) |
| House of Sport | 19 | 35 / 3.8 | 41 | ~100–125K sq ft flagship (climbing wall, turf, HitTrax cages, Trackman bays, premium footwear deck, Collectors Club House) |
| Golf Galaxy (incl Perf Centers) | 109 | 113 (33 GGPC) / 2.5 | 114 (37 GGPC) | GGPC = Trackman + service |
| Going Going Gone! | 21 (+29 Warehouse Sale restated in) | 51 / 2.3 | 52 | clearance outlet; also cleared FL inv in FY25 |
| Public Lands | 3 | 3 / 0.1 | 3 | outdoor (effectively wound down) |
| Total DICK'S Business | 856 (885 restated) | 888 / 45.5 | 892 / 46.0 | |
- Mgmt unit economics (Nov-2025 investor deck): HoS ~120K sq ft, Yr-1 omni sales ~$35M, Yr-1 4-wall omni EBITDA ~$7M (~20%), net capex "slightly over $20M", net inv ~$4M / pre-opening ~$3.5M, cash-on-cash ~25% / payback <4 yrs. Field House ~50K sq ft, ~$14M, ~$3M (~20%), ~$4.5M, ~$2M/~$1M, ~40% / ~2.5 yrs. Deck footnote: sales gross of cannibalization; returns blend remodel, lease, purchased deal structures. Wells (2026-08-17 primer Exh. 28): HoS 4-wall ROIC 27.2%, payback 3.7 yrs at 47.5% merch margin and 124% of legacy-store productivity; ROIC 9.9% if sales $25M not $35M. Returns slide not repeated after Nov-2025; "75 to 100 House of Sport by end-2027" target disappeared from FY25 10-K and FY26 10-Qs; announced openings reach ~67 by end-2027 (~35 FYE25 + ~14 FY26 + ~18 under construction for 2027; derived). Stack: 67–68 vs 75 "a pretty meaningless difference" (JPM Forum 2026-04-08).
- Relocations stay in comp base: FY2024 only 1 of 7 HoS and 4 of 15 Field House "openings" net-new; ~75% of FY26 openings relocations/remodels (FY25 10-K). Relocated store's sales count in comps from original open date (FY2022 change) → +100% volume after HoS conversion adds to comp. Wells: relocations +200–275bp/yr to DICK'S comp, GameChanger +20–40bp → "underlying" core comp +1.0–1.5% FY26–28E (2.8% FY24, 1.9% FY25 by Wells disaggregation; Exh. 17/39). UBS: HoS ~150bp (~30%) of comp. Central debate.
- Category mix (% consolidated sales; 10-K):
| Category | FY21 | FY22 | FY23 | FY24 | FY25 (incl $3.1B FL) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hardlines | 44% | 40% | 38% | 36% | 29% |
| Apparel | 34% | 34% | 33% | 33% | 28% |
| Footwear | 21% | 24% | 26% | 28% | 40% |
| Other | 1% | 2% | 3% | 3% | 3% |
- PF full-yr FL: ~49% footwear, ~76% softlines (Wells Exh. 83). Footwear "the engine that pulls the train" (Hobart, Q2 FY25 call 2025-08-28). No category margin disclosure; vertical brands "700 to 900 basis points of higher margin compared to the national brands" (raised from "600 to 800" Nov-2024; Gupta Q1 FY25 call 2025-05-28); trading cards/licensed "slightly lower margin" (Gupta Q3 FY25 call 2025-11-25); hardgoods (golf, fitness) lower margin than apparel; GameChanger/DMN ~80% GM (UBS est). 2016–18 hunting exit (~$1B sales at ~1,700bp lower margin; Stack, Goldman 2025-09-04) = template for mix-led margin gains.
- Nike concentration (10-Ks): DKS ~23% FY22, ~24% FY23, ~25% FY24, ~31% consolidated purchases FY25 (incl ~5 mo FL). FL alone 75% (2020) → 68% (2021) → 65% (2022) → ~65% (2023) → ~59% (2024). Wells: ~37% of PF merch sales (Exh. 85). No LT contracts; short-term POs.
- Allocation = moat: 2017 vendor segmentation (strategic/transactional/other); return to top of Nike wholesale list (Nike >6pts of DSG growth 2022; Wells Exh. 20) explains DKS out-comping ASO by double digits; "brand access" primary reason ASO under-comps (Wells expert-call section). Premium footwear decks ~90% of stores (Gupta Q3 FY24).
- Vertical brands ~13% of DICK'S Business sales (~$1.8B FY25; 10-K risk factor line 679) — DKS's #2 "vendor"; 2024 goal $2B (Hobart, MS conf 2024-12-03). Share slipped ~14% → ~13% in FY2023.
- Allowances: markdown support, co-op ad part of merch margin; FY25 10-K added "markdown allowances" to vendor incentives "not guaranteed." Expert calls (Jun–Jul 2026): stable trade terms; DKS requires specific inbound margin; DKS self-funds markdowns on MAP brands w/o margin agreements (UGG).
- Omni: ~70% FY25 sales from omni athletes; 90%+ store-enabled; 80%+ online orders fulfilled from stores (Mar-2026 deck). E-com % not disclosed since 2021; Wells est ~15–16%; e-com profit-neutral after 2017 in-housing.
- ScoreCard: ~30M members, >75% of DICK'S Business sales; ~8M Gold >50% of sales (FY25 10-K). ScoreCard+ paid tier $99/yr launched July 1, 2026 (Q2 FY26 call).
- GameChanger: "nearly $150 million in revenue during fiscal 2025," ~10M unique active users (FY25 10-K); ~40% CAGR (Hobart Q4 FY25 call); in comps since FY2024.
- DMN: launched 4Q FY2024; ~20B impressions FY25 (Mar-2026 deck); "has now reached profitability" (UBS 2026-08-26); unquantified; Wells <1% of sales. Q2 FY26: part of +79bp DICK'S GM gain from DMN & GameChanger; JPM split IEEPA refunds ~50bp, DMN ~25bp, GameChanger ~35bp → core merch margin ex these ≈−30bp (derived).
- FY2025 segment P&L (10-K Note 17; $000; ratios derived):
| | DICK'S Business | % | FL (Sep 8–Jan 31) | % |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Net sales | 14,108,943 | 100% | 3,106,177 | 100% |
| Cost of merch & services (incl supply chain for FL) | 7,100,929 | 50.3% | 1,873,614 | 60.3% |
| Occupancy | 1,197,019 | 8.5% | 414,557 | 13.3% |
| Personnel | 1,972,850 | 14.0% | 497,616 | 16.0% |
| Other segment exp | 2,269,702 | 16.1% | 372,610 | 12.0% |
| Segment profit (loss) | 1,568,443 | 11.1% | (52,220) | (1.7)% |
- Read-across: DKS GM incl occupancy/distribution in COGS → GM moves w/ rent leverage; FL occupancy ~13% vs ~8.5% (mall-rent-heavy, comp declines deleverage fast); FL merch cost ~10pts worse — "allocation + markdown" gap Wells attributes mostly to Nike.
- Leases (10-K Note 8): op lease liab $5.84B (from $3.00B pre-FL); total lease cost $1.165B FY25; wtd remaining term 7.71 yrs; signed not commenced $756M. "Approximately three-quarters of our DICK'S Sporting Goods stores will be up for lease renewal at our option over the next five years." FL leases 5–10-yr initial terms (vs 10–15 DICK'S) → FL closures cheaper/faster.
- Capex: $364M FY22 → $587M → $803M → $1,137M gross FY25 → ~$1.6B gross / ~$1.4B net FY26 gdnc (~$1.0B DICK'S, ~$0.4B FL). D&A $488.6M FY25 → capex ~2.3x D&A (derived); HoS depreciation margin headwind for years (UBS 2026-03-13: "incremental D&A from HoS offsets GM gains").
- WC: inv turns ~2.8x; DICK'S inv +6% y/y Q2 FY26 "in line with our total sales growth" (Gupta 2026-08-25); FL inv $2.0B at 8/1/26 (from $1.7B Q1). Supply-chain finance liab $25.8M. Landlord construction allowances doubled to $161.7M FY25.
- FCF (CFO − gross capex; derived): FY22 $557.8M; FY23 $939.9M; FY24 $509.3M; FY25 $400.2M; 1H FY26 $48.8M; LTM to 8/1/26 $239.4M (424B2 2026-09-23 table). Dividends ~$450M/yr ($1.25/qtr × ~89M sh).
- Margin bridge (mgmt, Mar-2026 slides): DICK'S non-GAAP OM 5.22% (FY19) → 11.12% (FY25): +699bp GM (+328bp merch margin, +343bp fixed-cost/occupancy leverage, +28bp supply chain) less −109bp SG&A & pre-opening. Wells: GM ~700bp above 2019 (~360bp merch, ~310bp occupancy, ~30bp other), OM ~600bp higher; sales/sq ft ~$208 → ~$317 (Exh. 21).
- Q/Q swing factors: (1) merch margin (promo, clearance, full-price allocated, vertical mix, freight, shrink, tariffs/IEEPA refunds): expansion decelerated +84bp Q3 FY24 → +18bp Q2 FY25 → +5bp Q3 FY25 → negative Q1 FY26 (~−29bp derived); Q2 FY26 +137bp "due primarily to IEEPA tariff refunds attributable to tariff costs incurred in the current year" (Q2 FY26 10-Q). (2) Occupancy leverage — comp-driven; new DC (Fort Worth, April 2026), HoS rent. (3) SG&A — labor, marketing (World Cup Q2 FY26), tech, incentive comp, healthcare; levered 1 of 7 DICK'S qtrs Q3 FY24–Q2 FY26; mgmt leverage point "low single digits comp" (Gupta 2025-08-28). (4) Pre-opening ~$90M FY26 gdnc, peaks Q3. (5) Below line: interest income falling with cash; deferred-comp marks (EBT-neutral); tax rate 25.5% → 27% → 29% FY26 gdnc (FL Europe losses no tax benefit).
- DICK'S segment margin y/y: −55bp Q3 FY25 (8.92% vs 9.47%); +88bp Q4 FY25 (10.97% vs 10.09%, only qtr SG&A levered); −66bp Q1 FY26 (10.69% vs 11.35%); −42bp Q2 FY26 (12.60% vs 13.02%); Q2 FY26 ≈−96bp ex ~54bp current-yr IEEPA refund (derived). Despite comps +5.7%, +6.0%, +4.9%. FY26 DICK'S segment margin gdnc 10.6–10.9% = 20–50bp below FY25 11.12%.

### 2. Industry & value chain
- Size: "$150–250B US market (depending on definition)" growing "+LSD" (Wells 2026-08-10). DKS ~$140B US core TAM, ~$300B global; ASO $245B incl outdoor/hunting/work. DKS ~9% US share 2025, +~50bp in 2025 (Mar-2026 deck).
- Euromonitor US sporting goods & athletic apparel/footwear retail share (B&M; Wells Exh. 82):
| | 2016 | 2019 | 2021 | 2023 | 2025 | Δ16–25 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DSG | 15.2% | 17.1% | 18.2% | 17.5% | 19.7% | +446bp |
| Foot Locker | 9.6% | 10.0% | 8.0% | 6.3% | 5.9% | −368bp |
| DKS+FL | 24.9% | 27.1% | 26.2% | 23.8% | 25.7% | +79bp |
| Academy | 10.0% | 10.8% | 11.8% | 9.6% | 9.3% | −65bp |
| JD/Pentland (JD, Finish Line, Hibbett) | 4.8% | 6.3% | 8.3% | 8.2% | 9.1% | +435bp |
| Bass Pro + Cabela's | 13.2% | 13.8% | 13.1% | 12.0% | 11.7% | −150bp |
| REI | 4.5% | 5.9% | 4.4% | 3.7% | 3.6% | −94bp |
| Others | 42.6% | 36.2% | 36.2% | 42.7% | 40.6% | −205bp |
- DKS organic gain ~offset by FL loss; combined ≈2016 level. JD built share by acquisition (Finish Line 2018, Hibbett 2024).
- Amazon ~13% of traditional sporting goods sales, flat ~decade; category e-com penetration ~40% (E-Marketer via Wells Exh. 89). Barriers: bulky items, try-on, premium brands limiting distribution.
- Consolidation: 27 events since 2003 (Sports Authority liquidation 2016; Bass Pro/Cabela's ~$5B; JD/Finish Line ~$558M; JD/Hibbett ~$1.1B at ~6x EBITDA; DKS/FL $2.5B EV at ~0.3x sales, ~6–7x depressed EBITDA — "the defining event of the cycle") (Wells Exh. 80).
- Demand: 23% of Americans do sports/exercise daily (ATUS all-time high); youth 6–17 participation 58% 2024 vs 49% 2021; avg spend on child's primary sport $1,016 (+46% vs 2019); record 181M outdoor participants; record 77M gym members (Wells pp.42–50). Price elasticity −1.35 (= children's clothing); historically deflationary (prices fell 83% of months since 2000); 2026 YTD prices +3.5% (tariff pass-through), units +6.0% (Wells Exh. 94–98). Census sporting-goods retail sales −1–4%/yr 2022–25, +7% YTD 2026 (Exh. 99).
- Nike cycle: 2017–2023 "Consumer Direct Acceleration" cut wholesale doors; FL "high heat" allocation ~40% → ~20% (Wells channel checks); FL Nike high-heat sales ~$1.6B (FY21) → ~$0.7B (FY23) (Exh. 8). Under CEO Elliott Hill (from Oct-2024) back to wholesale: NA wholesale rev +11%, +24%, +11%, +9.8% (derived), +9% Nike Q1 FY26–Q1 FY27; FY26 NA wholesale $11,958M (+14%) vs NA Direct $8,553M (−6%). Nike top-3 US customers ~29% of US sales (from ~25%) (FY26 10-K).
- Sell-in vs sell-through: Needham: Nike Nov-2025 qtr "the largest period of domestic wholesale sell-in" in its history (~$1.5B more wholesale ≈ ~$3B at retail). By summer 2026 lifestyle franchises (AF1, Dunk, Jordan 1, Samba) clogging channel; >30% of Nike assortment discounted across 14 wholesale partners (25% yr earlier). Nike guided FY27 rev down HSD, ~400bp extra 2Q pressure lapping "higher North America sell-in"; Sportswear/Jordan/China resets "beyond fiscal 2027"; BBG cons NA wholesale −10.4% c-n Sep–Nov 2026 (≈DKS Q3), −4.8% Dec–Feb.
- Performance vs lifestyle: running (Vomero/Pegasus, HOKA, On, ASICS, New Balance), "brown shoe" (UGG, Birkenstock), women's basketball strong; legacy lifestyle weak. DKS advantaged (performance skew, less launch-dependent): "Foot Locker is more dependent on launch and retro product" (Stack 2026-08-25).
- Mall: DICK'S ~30% of stores in malls (Hobart Q1 FY25); HoS A-mall access (Palm Beach Gardens, Cerritos, Tysons). FL ~74% mall-based in NA at FY2022; off-mall share NA gross sq ft 34% → 39% → 42% (FY22–24); ~35% FL mall stores in C/D malls (Wells); >75% of Champs within a mile of a Foot Locker.
- Academy: comps negative every yr since FY2022; Q1 FY26 +2.9% (all ticket; transactions −1.5%); Q2 FY26 −0.4% (transactions −5.3%) same weeks DKS +4.9%. ASO footwear −1.0% Q2; ex one-time IEEPA refund GM ~−70bp (partly reinvesting refund in price). ASO added 55 Jordan shop concepts cal Q2 2026 (UBS); HOKA entered 15 Academy doors — brand access broadening down-market.
- JD Sports (Finish Line/Hibbett): NA LFL ~−7% mid-2026; JPM models −6%/−5% Q3/Q4 → negative FL read, neutral-positive DICK'S share.
- Brand DTC: On wants ~75% of key-partner doors (DKS, FL, JD named) by 2029 (Investor Day 2026-09-22) while DTC grows faster; DECK: sporting goods/athletic specialty = HOKA main US whitespace ("several key wholesale partners remain underpenetrated, particularly in athletic specialty"). UBS: investor worry Nike moving into lower-end doors (Famous Footwear Jordan styles +63%; possible Decathlon) — dilutes DKS/FL preferred status.
- Others: Walmart/Target/Amazon, off-price at value end; SCHEELS only comparable experiential competitor; run specialty for pinnacle running.
- Gaining: DKS core (comp +4.9% vs ASO −0.4%, JD NA ~−7%); brand DTC (On DTC +34%, HOKA DTC +17%); adidas (World Cup, shelf space from Nike per DB channel checks). Losing: FL, Academy (traffic), JD NA, dept stores, Nike lifestyle.

### 3. Macro (ranked by est. 12-mo EPS impact; author judgment)
| # | Factor | Direction | Transmission | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Athletic footwear channel inv/promos (Nike sell-in) | Negative thru Q4 FY26, improving into Spring 2027 | footwear ~40% FY25 sales, ~49% PF; excess lifestyle inv → markdowns at DICK'S & FL, lower FL AUR | Nike NA wholesale +14% FY26; Nov-2025 "largest period"; >30% discounted vs 25%; ~400bp 2Q FY27 pressure; Stack 2026-08-25 footwear "hangover", promotional "particularly in footwear" |
| 2 | Tariffs & IEEPA refunds | Mixed; cost relief likely; FY27 laps refunds | Merch margin. ~$59.0M refunds Q2 FY26 ($57M DICK'S, $2M FL); $38.1M prior-yr excluded from non-GAAP; ~$21M ($19M DICK'S) in non-GAAP merch margin, "reinvested … to remain competitively priced" | BI 2026-10-02 "Tariff refunds continue to cloud earnings comparability"; Barclays 2026-03-09 relief likely "following the SCOTUS ruling"; Lasser (Q2 FY26 call): FY27 must lap "World Cup … the Knicks win … tariff refunds" |
| 3 | Low/mid-income squeeze: gas, "Iran war" energy shock, rates | Negative at margin | traffic/units > ticket; ASO Q2 transactions −5.3%; JPM 2026-07-02 low-income gas pressure "echoed in recent reporting" of DKS & ASO | DB checks 2026-07-22: one US retailer July "down double digits" (gas, weather); BI 2026-10-01 possible Fed hikes (flipped from "Fed cuts" assumed Jan–Mar 2026); DKS dropped "growth across all income demographics … no signs of trading down" (every call Nov-2024–May-2026) in Aug-2026 |
| 4 | FIFA World Cup (Jun–Jul 2026) | Tailwind 1H FY26, headwind FY27 compares | team sports/soccer comp; marketing SG&A (adidas partnership); 1H FY26 comps higher "primarily due to the FIFA World Cup" | adidas: WC sales "not fully incremental" |
| 5 | Youth sports participation/spend | Structural tailwind | team sports, GameChanger, HoS traffic | 58% vs 49%; $1,016 (+46% vs 2019) |
| 6 | Weather | Noise (Q4 cold matters most) | outerwear, hunting-adjacent, fleece; Q4 FY25 helped by cold Northeast (Needham: NYC ~4–5°F colder Oct–Dec 2025) | DB/Planalytics 3Q weather files = webinar invites, no data |
| 7 | FX/Europe | Negative for FL | FL intl ~30–33% of FL rev (mostly EMEA; Wells/BI) loss-making; no tax benefit under $288.5M valuation allowance → ETR ~28–29% | Q2 FY26 ETR 28.0% vs 25.2%; Gupta: EMEA "promotional environment remains very aggressive … the industry is carrying too much inventory" (2026-08-25) |
| 8 | Credit markets | Modest negative | new notes 6.200%/6.900% vs legacy 3.704% wtd coupon (derived) | BI "pressure point" (2026-09-22); "Rating risk could emerge for … Dick's Sporting Goods … despite stable outlooks from raters" (2026-09-30); DKS 2052 bonds 134bp G-spread vs 102bp pre-cut |
| 9 | GLP-1, Amazon, China | Low | GLP-1 raised by Civello (Q1 FY26 call), unquantified; Amazon flat ~13%; China only via Nike/adidas | — |
- Doesn't matter much: housing/big-ticket credit (no consumer credit book); cotton/freight (Barclays Mar 2026: costs "lower … from freight, cotton, polyesters").
- BI card-spend pack "Sporting Goods Spending Extends Hot Streak" (2026-08-28) has no numbers; conflicts w/ JD NA "significantly softer sell through" (2026-08-20), DB July checks. Census +7% YTD 2026 (price +3.5%, units +6.0%). Not demand collapse; mix & promos — Stack: "I don't think this is a demand issue" (2026-08-25).

### 4. History, mgmt, governance, capital allocation
- Timeline: 1948 Dick Stack bait-and-tackle shop Binghamton NY; 1984 Ed Stack takes over (2 stores); 2002 IPO; 2016–18 exits hunting (~$1B sales at ~1,700bp lower margin) and gun sales in many stores after Parkland (Feb-2018), Nike vendor segmentation reset; 2021-02-01 Hobart CEO, Stack Exec Chair; FY20–21 COVID boom sales ~$8.75B (FY19) → $12.29B (FY21), OM ~5% → ~16%; 2021-04 first HoS (Victor, NY); 2022-01 $750M 3.15% 2032 + $750M 4.10% 2052 notes; FY22–23 converts settled, buybacks $426.7M (FY22), $648.6M (FY23), dividend +105% to $1.00/qtr (Mar-2023); 2024-01-02 Stack first approaches Dillon; 2024-11-15 DKS walks at $37–40; 2025-03-10 new $3B 5-yr buyback, dividend +10% to $1.2125; 2025-05-15 FL deal $24.00 (cash or 0.1168 DKS); 2025-09-08 close; 2026-01-28 DKS buys $49.0M aircraft from Ed Stack's EWS IV, LLC (related party; FY25 10-K Note 18); 2026-03-11 FY26 guide EPS $13.50–14.50, dividend +3% to $1.25; 2026-08-25 cut to $11.00–12.00, stock −30.7%; 2026-09-22/25 $1B 2036/2056 notes priced/settled.
- Ed Stack (Exec Chair, Class B controller): 2 stores → ~890; architect of Nike allocation strategy, 2018 gun decision; personally runs FL integration ("DICK'S Executive Chairman, Ed Stack, will lead the global Foot Locker businesses," 8-K 2025-11-25); co-CODM. FY24 10-K "Executive Chairman and Chief Merchant"; FY25 drops "Chief Merchant". Track record: strong core turnaround 2017–21; weak on FL so far — "accretive to EPS in the first full fiscal year post-close" (2025-05-15), later called FY26 FL guide "conservative," then cut to loss. Exercised 958,466 options 2026-06-24, didn't sell; didn't buy after crash. "Foot Locker strayed from Retail 101 and did not execute the fundamentals" (Q3 FY25 call 2025-11-25).
- Lauren Hobart (Pres & CEO since Feb-2021): core 4%+ comps most of FY23–FY26. Exercised & sold 20,083 sh for $4.58M at $227.69–228.34 on 2026-05-28 (day after Q1 print, 3 mo before cut). 10b5-1 adopted 2026-06-05 covering 24,182 sh, window opens 9/4/26. Didn't buy after crash.
- Navdeep Gupta (CFO since 2021; ex-Pepsi/Kraft): bought $999,983 on 2026-09-22 (same day notes priced). Owns guidance credibility problem: GM language change Aug-2025 ("75bp" → "up"); held FY26 guide May-2026 then cut Aug.
- FL leadership (new since close): Ann Freeman, President FL North America since Sept-2025, ex-Nike NA GM, ex-Finish Line.
- Matthew Barnes, President FL International from 2025-12-03, ex-Aldi Süd, ex-Tesco UK CEO; bought $499,869 stock Sept-2026.
- Succession: FY25 10-K risk factor names succession planning "including Ms. Hobart and Mr. Stack." Michael Stack (Ed's son) attended Jan-2024 FL mtg.
- Credibility scorecard:
| Promise | Date | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| FL "accretive to EPS in the first full fiscal year post-close" | 2025-05-15 | FY26 guide implies FL loses $40–80M; accretion language gone after Nov-2025 |
| "75 to 100 House of Sport by end-2027" | Nov-2024 | dropped from filings; ~67 announced by end-2027 |
| FY26 EPS $13.50–14.50 | 2026-03-11 | held 2026-05-28; cut to $11–12 2026-08-25 |
| FY26 FL OI $100–150M | 2026-03-11 | $110–150M (May), then −$80 to −$40M (Aug) |
| $100–125M cost synergies, medium term | 2025-05 | unchanged every filing; none quantified as captured |
| Core FY26 DICK'S comp 2.5–4.0% | 2026-03 | maintained; 1H comps +6.0%, +4.9% |
| FY2025 DICK'S guide (EPS raised 3x) | 2025 | delivered core EPS $14.58 vs $14.05 |
- Verdict: core under-promise/over-deliver since 2021; FL wrong twice in 9 mo (price path, FY26 guide). Street discounts FL gdnc, trusts core gdnc — asymmetry matters for Nov-2026 print.

### 4.3 Governance
- Dual class: 23,570,633 Class B (10 votes) vs 65,474,242 common (2026-03-20); Class B ~26% of shares (23.57/89.04) but ~78% of votes (235.7M/(235.7M+65.5M)) (derived). June-2025 charter amendment raised authorized Class B 40M → 200M — largest opposition at 2025 AGM (For 238.1M / Against 39.7M). 2026 8-K added "the potential future issuance of Class B common stock" to risk factors.
- Change of control: 2036/2056 indenture "Permitted Person" = Edward W. Stack + Affiliated Parties "incl. family to first cousin" → Stacks can't trigger CoC put.
- Dissent rising: withhold vs Ed Stack 1.67M → 10.77M (2025 → 2026 AGM held 2026-06-10, pre-cut). Anne Fink highest withhold 2025 (18.0M). Larry Stone left slate 2026 (11 nominees vs 12). Say-on-pay 280.9M For / 2.5M Against. Class B 10 votes → withhold % understates public dissent.
- Related party: 2026-01-28 aircraft $49.0M from EWS IV, LLC (Stack-owned); audit cttee approved on independent valuation; aircraft previously used under leases w/ Stack LLCs (Note 18). Not material; governance flag.
- Insider buying post-crash: 7 insiders 42,795 sh, $5,588,663, VWAP $130.59, 2026-08-26 to 2026-09-30: Director Barrenechea $2.22M; CFO Gupta $999,983 (9/22); FL Intl Pres Barnes $499,869. CEO & Exec Chair didn't buy. 2026 pre-crash insider sales $5.51M at $220–228 (Hobart $4.58M on 5/28).
- Holders: Wellington 11.7% of common; Sachem Head, Darsana, Maverick (+41% position change); short interest 8.0M sh, 13.7% of float, +29%.

S-16): cash $913,736K → $1,902,155K as adjusted; sr notes carrying $1,872,159K → $2,860,559K; net debt ex-leases unchanged ~$958M until cash spent (derived); total unsecured debt incl ~$6.1B op leases ≈$13.5B PF; $2.0B revolver due 2030 undrawn, max total-leverage covenant (level undisclosed).

Timing tell: 1 mo after 30% drawdown, same day CFO bought ~$1M, buybacks in use of proceeds. Credit mkts read leverage-up (BI "pressure point"; 2052s 134bp G-spread). Mgmt silent; Q3 call (Nov 24/25) first chance.

### 5. Foot Locker acquisition
- Stated logic (2025-05-15 deal call, 424B3): (i) enter global athletic-specialty/sneaker-culture mkt (~$300B global TAM), urban/mall, younger, more diverse customer; (ii) intl expansion (DKS US-only; "interest in global expansion" from Jan 2024); (iii) "Retail 101" expertise + brand relationships; (iv) $100–125M medium-term cost synergies (procurement, direct sourcing); (v) FL standalone unit, "maintain the Foot Locker brand," banners separate. Stack: "decisions … for a lifetime" (Q1 FY25 call 2025-05-28).
- Unstated logic: Nike's wholesale return makes FL (largest sneaker-specialty partner) more valuable to Nike; scale w/ Nike across two customer bases; ~0.3x sales price on depressed margins. 424B3 FL board concern re "the recently announced CEO succession at an important Foot Locker brand partner" (Sept-2024); Nike never named.
- Price path (=same as SRC 01 §2): 2024-01-02/04 approach & Chicago mtg; 2024-01-11 FL declines (Lace Up); 2024-04-23 JD/Hibbett, Stack messages Dillon same day; 2024-06-07 $31–34; Jul–Sep 2024 diligence, Palm Beach, FL asks $41, DKS $37 then "$40 best and final"; 2024-11-15 walks; 2025-03 re-approach, threatens public, ~4.5% toehold (4.3M sh), $24.00; 2025-04-02/03 Liberation-Day tariffs FL −15.4%; 2025-04-25 $25.50 (80% stock cap); 2025-05-01 Stack "very concerned" → cut to $22; 2025-05-14/15 signed at $24.00 cash or 0.1168 DKS. Company cited ~66% premium to 60-day VWAP and ~6.1x FY24 adj EBITDA (FL FY24 adj EBITDA $395M, Nov-25 deck); proxy/Evercore 80.3% to 5/13 close, 6.4x TTM; 86.5% cash premium to $12.87 May-14 close (derived) (reconciliation A7, D26). 2025-09-08 closed (HSR cleared; FL holders approved).
- Process critique: no market check; 9/29/24 decision not to contact strategics; Company X wanted minority only; DKS twice threatened public; Evercore consensus comps $10.25–20.45, mgmt DCF $20.95–30.90, downside DCF $17.00–25.05 ("for information purposes only"); at $24 FL holders well paid vs standalone = mirror of DKS-holder problem.
- Consideration total $2,512,662K (FY25 10-K Note 2): DKS sh issued 9,579,640 (equity stmt 9,580K sh, $2,144,211K); cash $222.96M; FV prior stake $111.6M (4,269,998 sh, 4.47%, cost $69.5M, ~$16.16/sh derived; cancelled for no consideration); replacement awards $29,032K.
- Election: stock 85.8% (final); prelim 92.6%. Ex-FL holders own 9.6M sh (~11%) of DKS.
- PPA ($K) Q3 FY25 first → Q2 FY26 latest: goodwill 458,442 → 599,846; tradenames (indefinite) 740,000 → 710,000; PP&E 756,906 → 709,017; inventory 1,718,069 → 1,718,069; op lease ROU 1,974,776 → 1,876,206; valuation allowance on FL DTAs $288.5M (unchanged disclosure). $54.3M of $204.3M interchange settlement attributed to FL, booked vs goodwill. Only litigation contingencies open.
- Impairment risk: ~$591M FL goodwill (carrying after FX) + $710M tradenames vs FL guided FY26 loss; annual test likely Q4 → non-cash write-down plausible at Q4 FY26 print March 2027 (author inference).
- FL OM FY2018–FY2025 (Wells Exh. 9): 9.3% / 8.9% / 5.6% / 11.6% / 7.9% / 2.7% / 2.5% / (1.3)%; Wells: ~490bp of 880bp decline from FY21 = lost Nike allocation. Nike share of FL purchases =same as §1.4; ~57% per Wells 2025.
- Lace Up (Dillon, Investor Day 2023-10) vs actual: EBIT margin 8.5–9.0% by 2026 (later 2028) vs adj EBIT ~2.6% (FY23), ~2.4% (FY24), ~(0.2)% LTM pre-close; comps +3.5–4.5%/yr 2025–28 vs FY23 −7.0%, FY24 +2.6%, Q1 FY25 −2.6%; close ~400 stores/open "community"/"power" formats vs store count fell, WSS & Champs pruned, EMEA losses grew.
- Proxy mgmt case =same as SRC 01 §3 (rev $8,000M → $8,986M; adj EBITDA $453M → $830M, 5.7% → 9.2%; downside $432M → $731M; Street below mgmt case).
- Post-close FL segment:
| | Q3 FY25 stub (9/8–11/1) | Q4 FY25 | Q1 FY26 | Q2 FY26 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Comp (PF) | −4.7% | −3.4% | +0.6% | −3.6% |
| Segment profit | $(46.3)M | $(5.9)M | $17.5M | $(31.9)M |
| Owned stores end | 2,347 | — | — | 2,212 |
- Q4 FY25 GM guided down 1,000–1,500bp for clearance (Barclays 2026-03-09). FL Q2 FY26 sales $1,736.9M, −6.5% y/y vs Q2 FY25 PF $1,857.1M (derived).
- Restructuring: gdnc "$500 million to $750 million" (Q3 FY25) → "up to $750 million" (Q2 FY26); $390.0M FY25 (incl $217.9M inventory write-down, "clean out the garage") + $125.8M 1H FY26 = $515.8M to date; incl Organizational Alignment $50–55M. 67 unproductive stores closed by Q2 FY26; WSS 151 at acquisition → 99 (−34% derived).
- Integration: "Fast Break" store reset ~100 stores by Q1 FY26, >250 by BTS 2026, 300–350 by YE; vertical brands into FL ("way too soon to tell"); purchase-term synergies "none yet" (Gupta Q1 FY26); FL capex $140.2M 1H FY26, ~$0.4B FY26.
- Latest framing: Stack on rightsizing/monetizing parts of FL: "simply yes" (Q2 FY26 call 2026-08-25); Sept conference "creating a menu" of options, "no preconceived ideas" (2026-09-14); "Foot Locker is more dependent on launch and retro product"; EMEA "more challenging than expected."
- Original targets vs now:
| Item | At announcement / Mar-2026 | Now |
|---|---|---|
| EPS accretion | "accretive ... first full fiscal year post-close" (FY26) | FY26 EPS gdnc $11–12 vs FY25 DICK'S-only non-GAAP $14.58 ≈ −20% dilutive (derived) |
| FY26 FL OI | $100–150M (Mar), $110–150M (May) | −$80M to −$40M (Aug) |
| FY26 FL PF comps | +1.0% to +3.0% (Mar), +1.5% to +3.0% (May) | −2.0% to 0% (Aug) |
| FY26 FL net sales | $7.6–7.7B (Mar/May) | $7.4–7.5B (Aug) |
| Synergies | $100–125M medium term | unchanged, none quantified |
| Charges | $500–750M | up to $750M; $515.8M to date |
| Price paid | $2.51B (~$2.4B EV incl ~$0.4B debt less ~$0.5B cash; derived) | implied ≈ −$0.8B to −$1.2B (SOTP derived) |
- Exit/option economics (author framework; no banner asset disclosure): FL NA (FL, Kids, Champs, WSS) ~67–70% of FL sales; intl ~30–33% of FL FY2024 rev (Wells; BI 07/23/26); owns Nike/Jordan relationship & Fast Break upside = core of keep case. FL EMEA (most of intl; APAC/atmos rest): EMEA comps −10.2%/−11.4% in FL Q1/Q2 FY2025; loss-making, valuation-allowed tax; licensing exit (licensed stores Europe/ME/Asia model exists per Q3 FY25 10-Q) removes losses + ~4pts tax drag ≈ 4pts × ~$1.45B pretax ≈ $58M ≈ $0.65/sh (derived); cash exit costs unknown, European closure/labor costs typically high. WSS (Hispanic-family value banner, ~99 stores) & atmos (Japan streetwear) small; sale/closure candidates. Capex: ~$0.4B FY26 discretionary; maintenance ~$0.15–0.2B frees ~$0.2B/yr (derived).
- Bottom line: paid ~$2.5B, $516M charges booked; market assigns FL ≈ −$1B (derived); requires indefinite cash burn or core impairment; FL 2018–21 history, Nike wholesale pivot, short FL leases (5–10 yrs) → credible "rightsize and simplify" makes −$1B too pessimistic; execution/timing risk.

- Leverage: net debt ex-leases ≈$958M ≈0.45x FY26E EBITDA ~$2.14B (BBG EBITDA $2,143.1M; derived); incl leases BBG net debt $7,049M ≈3.3x (mixed bases); Baa2/BBB stable; BI "Rating risk could emerge" (2026-09-30).

### 6.6 What has to be true for consensus
- FY26E cons $11.49 (slightly below $11–12 midpoint) requires: DICK'S 2H seg profit ~$704M (−4% y/y); FL 2H loss ~$57M; ~29% tax; ~90M sh w/ BBG only ~$138M 2H buybacks (Q3E $75.0M + Q4E $62.5M).
- FY27E cons $13.36 (+16.3%) on rev +2.3% (margin recovery). From BBG segment cons (derived): DICK'S OI $1,554.7M → $1,647.2M (+5.9%, +29bp margin on +3.0% sales); FL OI $(72.6)M → +$19.7M (≈+$92M); tax 28.59% → 27.46%; buybacks ~$275M.
- Obstacles: (a) lapping ~$21M current-yr IEEPA refunds in non-GAAP margin + World Cup + "Knicks" uplift (Lasser 2026-08-25); (b) ~$66M/yr new coupon (cons interest only $71.3M FY26E → $75.0M FY27E); (c) D&A growth from ~$1.4B/yr net capex; (d) FL margin repair while rev shrinks (−0.5%).
